# Nexus Operative versioning and launch gates

**Current development build: 0.340.75 (pre-release).** This build hardens explicit provider-read budget recovery by ignoring the append acknowledgement and requiring two identical cold read-only receipts under the same held provider ownership. A committed recovery with a lost acknowledgement succeeds once; uncommitted, fabricated, changed or unreadable state remains fixed uncertainty. It adds no Portal route, saved credential, provider IO, live endpoint or external authority. OFF defaults, limits, exits and the independent emergency stop remain unchanged. Rough implementation stays about 40% (30–45%, low confidence), with 0/9 fully accepted major stages.

## v0.340.75 provider-read recovery acknowledgement replay

Reviewed provider-read recovery now accepts success only after two identical read-only receipts prove the exact new recovery head, account digest, blocked session/outcome, incremented recovery count, unchanged cooldown, unchanged session count and false authority fields while provider ownership is held. The append return and exception detail are not trusted. A commit whose local acknowledgement is lost is therefore recovered once; an uncommitted or fabricated return and an unreadable or unstable receipt yield fixed `PAPER_PROVIDER_BUDGET_RECOVERY_DURABLE_STATE_CHANGED` uncertainty.

Exact-source v0.340.74 commit `1d8fb9aa77655fa3b7b20c295e5c8d57169d0496` passed public security/Gitleaks run 41188385670, every regression/browser/evidence job in run 41188385637 and Windows EXE/installer/reinstall/uninstall run 41188385616. Fresh v0.340.75 workflows and actual authorized paper-account/private-volume acceptance remain required. This does not prove provider source, broker-wide/distributed coordination, native-owner Windows acceptance, settled funds, fee/hold reconciliation, routing authority, market realism, profitability, cloud readiness or release acceptance.

Local prepublication validation passed 4,707 tests with one expected non-Windows skip and one existing warning; the provider-read recovery/release/evidence/version/installer focus passed 250. Sixteen offline profiles and saved evidence verified against 395 source files / `e7e13c612a6d5999c0b1c9c2111f8a39cd7642b2382b34ae2f8544e65689f3e5`. Evidence SHA-256 is `4cba3afeb308dbc7bc48d3ec6445305664f343f9dbc2dec9519aada2ad651546`; wheel SHA-256 is `b2e1da09a8c4809b7849789c309c0c6e8dcd34458e2192fc044724563799105d`. Compilation, dependency integrity, deterministic roadmap output, fourteen Node controller checks and Portal syntax passed. Local Playwright and Gitleaks are unavailable. Release approval and trading permission remain false.

## v0.340.74 read-ownership exit rechecks

Account-read and shared provider-read ownership now recheck the exact opened regular single-link lock identity during context exit. Path replacement, disappearance, symlink substitution or an inaccessible-path sampling failure yields fixed `OWNER_FILE_CHANGED` uncertainty on normal return and exception unwind while preserving descriptor cleanup. Provider POST ownership uses the same fixed normalization. POSIX controls exercise real replacement; Windows controls exercise the equivalent late failure without weakening its native mandatory-share lock.

## v0.340.73 Windows-portable ownership-exit controls

The production provider POST ownership exit validation is unchanged. On POSIX, negative controls still replace the open lock pathname and prove fixed `PAPER_PROVIDER_POST_BUDGET_OWNER_FILE_CHANGED`. On Windows, the mandatory-share lock correctly rejects that rename with WinError 32, so the controls deterministically inject the equivalent late identity failure and preserve both ordinary-return and exception-unwinding error priority. Recovery remains conservative and creates no retry, resend or routing authority.

Exact-source v0.340.72 commit `e03c1c73bb7aa1358052f1af56f11c39328ee7ac` passed public security run 41180421277 plus the browser and saved-evidence jobs in research run 41180421238. Fresh v0.340.73 workflows and actual authorized paper-account acceptance remain required. This does not prove broker acceptance, native-owner Windows acceptance, settled funds, fee/hold reconciliation, routing authority, market realism, profitability, cloud readiness or release acceptance.

## v0.340.69 recovery store-fingerprint bracket

Recovery fingerprints the bounded provider-budget bytes and metadata before source replay and requires that exact fingerprint after source inspection, history replay and immediately before the guarded write. It then samples the committed fingerprint and requires it around both cold receipts. A same-inode rewrite to valid older bytes yields `PAPER_PROVIDER_POST_RECOVERY_FILE_SET_CHANGED`. This is a sampled local bracket, not an atomic descriptor transaction, ACL or administrator/power-loss guarantee.

## v0.340.68 recovery store-identity fence

Recovery samples the existing provider-budget file identity before source replay and requires that exact regular single-link file after source inspection, history replay, ownership acquisition, the guarded recovery attempt and each cold receipt. An otherwise valid replacement database cannot masquerade as the committed recovery and yields `PAPER_PROVIDER_POST_RECOVERY_FILE_IDENTITY_CHANGED`. This is a sampled path fence, not an ACL or administrator/power-loss guarantee.

## v0.340.66 response-observation completion bracket

Successful `RESPONSE_OBSERVED` completion now replays the exact immutable request observation again after the provider-budget terminal row is cold-replayed. All event-binding fields must match the pre-write receipt; only the independently reported current request head may advance through later valid original-ID lookup history. A missing, invalidated or unreadable post-write observation yields `POST_BUDGET_COMPLETION_UNCERTAIN`, consumes no additional provider IO and never re-arms the request.

## v0.340.65 safety-closure request-head bracket

Every exceptional paper provider-budget closure now brackets its terminal write with stable complete request-journal receipts. The exact request head, account and payload must match before and after the cross-journal write. A concurrent lookup advance yields `POST_BUDGET_SAFETY_CLOSURE_UNCERTAIN`, preserves lookup-only recovery and never repeats provider IO. The same boundary covers zero-POST `NOT_SENT` and one-POST `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` while retaining pre-marker closure.

## v0.340.64 terminal completion-window binding

Provider-budget completion replay now validates the terminal row's retained `completed_at` in addition to its token, account/client hashes, request/payload heads, outcome and actual POST count. Successful response completion is bracketed by caller checkpoints. `NOT_SENT` and `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` closures use the last trusted timestamp exactly, preserving emergency-stop independence even when the triggering failure is revoked permission, an active stop, clock failure or transport uncertainty. A forged or out-of-window committed row remains fixed completion uncertainty and cannot cause provider IO.

## v0.340.63 durable admission-window binding

Provider-budget `BEGIN` replay now exposes its retained `admitted_at` value from the validated hash chain and proves it lies between caller-session samples that independently recheck permission, emergency stop, store bindings and monotonic clocks. The original ownership lease is still mandatory. Request-marker replay independently proves its canonical `evaluated_at` lies inside the marker's witnessed caller window, and the continuing reservation fence requires the provider admission not to follow the marker. A forged or out-of-window provider record remains pending before a marker; a forged or out-of-window marker is closed as `NOT_SENT` with zero POSTs. Neither case retries provider IO.

## v0.340.61 exact request-marker acknowledgement replay

The single-attempt boundary now treats `BrokerRequestJournal.begin_submission_checked` as transport rather than durable evidence. It ignores returns and raised acknowledgements, performs two read-only full-chain replays and requires one stable current marker whose canonical admission binds the exact prepared-request head and payload. A committed append with a lost local acknowledgement therefore continues without repeating the append; a missing, fabricated, changing or later-advanced marker fails as `PAPER_SUBMISSION_REQUEST_MARKER_DURABLE_STATE_CHANGED` before provider POST. The already reserved provider budget is closed conservatively, with no automatic retry or resend authority.

Exact-source v0.340.60 commit `0f3b605d8f2ae84e08e033b28f2e2f12e9883ac8` passed public security run 41117103498 and research/browser/evidence run 41117103398. ## v0.340.60 exact provider-budget reservation cold replay

The single-attempt boundary now treats the `PaperProviderPostBudget.begin` result as transport rather than durable evidence. It ignores returns and raised acknowledgements, performs two read-only replays of the complete journal and requires one stable pending receipt whose token equals the current head and whose hashed account/client, request-before head, payload digest and owner identity match the prepared submission. The exact original in-process lease must also remain active. A committed append with a lost local acknowledgement therefore continues without repeating the append; a missing, fabricated, changing or replacement-owner reservation fails as `PAPER_SUBMISSION_POST_BUDGET_RESERVATION_UNCERTAIN` before the request marker or provider POST.

Exact-source v0.340.59 commit `a332839bfc10135316f9e088ee8027d51f1c3d8b` passed public security run 41106267358, research/browser/evidence run 41106267368 and Windows EXE/installer/reinstall/uninstall run 41106267351. ## v0.340.59 exact failure-path budget closure replay

After any exception following the durable provider-budget reservation, the boundary writes the conservative zero-POST `NOT_SENT` or one-POST `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` result and ignores the in-process acknowledgement. Two stable read-only state/history replays must prove the exact token, account/client hashes, request-before and request-after heads, payload digest, outcome and actual POST count before the original fixed submission failure is preserved. A committed result whose acknowledgement was lost completes locally without retry. Pre-commit failure, fabricated returns and unstable replay yield `PAPER_SUBMISSION_POST_BUDGET_SAFETY_CLOSURE_UNCERTAIN` and do not re-arm the request.

## v0.340.58 exact response-observation cold replay

After the sole paper POST returns, the response observation is accepted only through two stable read-only journal receipts under the pinned binding. Each receipt validates the full current chain, finds the unique event whose previous head is the checked submission marker, requires its exact canonical `OBSERVE` body and payload digest, then replays the prefix through that sequence to reproduce the normalized observed snapshot. This preserves the exact observation head if a later valid lookup exists. A committed observation whose local acknowledgement was lost completes locally without retry; pre-commit failure or an uncommitted fabricated return retains one uncertain POST and the fixed `PAPER_SUBMISSION_POST_RESPONSE_DURABLE_STATE_CHANGED` error.

## v0.340.56 checked response-observation binding

After the single permitted paper POST returns, response observation succeeds only if one journal write transaction still sees the exact checked marker head, uncertain request state and payload digest. ## v0.340.26 request-journal fingerprint through POST recovery

The request-journal locked snapshot now samples identity plus bounded bytes and stable metadata before its write-excluding transaction. ## v0.340.25 request-journal writer exclusion during POST recovery

The resolved-unknown recovery boundary now acquires shared POST ownership and an exact-head request-journal write lock in the same order as submission. ## v0.340.24 cancelled-lifespan failure observation

When lifespan cancellation races with the private shutdown worker, the application now waits for worker completion, retrieves the concurrent future's terminal result and cancels the still-pending asyncio wrapper. ## v0.340.22 mandatory private lifecycle release evidence

The disposable real-loopback Uvicorn profile delegates to the exact application stop fence and records only whether exactly one fixed-prefix private lifecycle worker ran. ## v0.340.21 private lifecycle stop worker

The complete application stop fence runs in one private lifecycle executor. It drains any in-flight emergency cut before ordinary worker cleanup even when the shared default executor is occupied. If the lifecycle worker cannot be created or started, the identical nested fence runs synchronously instead of skipping cleanup. Direct regressions cover shared-pool starvation, worker-creation failure and the cancelled-caller cut-drain ordering exposed by hosted Windows load.

## v0.340.1 production fixed-diagnostic recovery match

The safety-closure recovery wrapper previously compared a typed error with an unprefixed code even though the production `_fail` boundary always emits the fixed `ALPACA_ACTIVITY_SSE_RECONNECT_BUDGET_` prefix. A closure whose terminal write committed and then raised its own typed acknowledgement-uncertainty diagnostic could therefore bypass the exact recovery attempted for a generic lost channel. The wrapper now matches only that complete fixed diagnostic before cold-replaying the exact requested terminal record. Other typed denials are still re-raised without recovery, so an older terminal state cannot override current confirmation, head, clock, stop or state failures.

## v0.313.0 bounded and link-safe paper recovery snapshots

The paper-portfolio snapshot boundary now samples the source as a regular single-link file before SQLite opens it and requires the same identity after backup. Data-root, source and recovery-directory symlinks are rejected. Each candidate uses exclusive no-follow creation, requests owner-only POSIX permissions, is rechecked as a regular single-link file and is hashed through a no-follow descriptor. ## v0.239.0 private crash-recoverable Activity SSE cursor journal

`ActivitySSECursorStore` accepts only a successful v0.238 supplied reconnect review whose account hash and previous/next cursor hashes match the caller's exact private values. ## v0.196.1 Windows timing-fixture handle closure

The chain-valid timing-envelope negative control now wraps its SQLite rewrite connection in `contextlib.closing`. Python's connection context manager handles transactions but does not close the connection, so exact-source v0.196.0 Windows cleanup could not delete the still-open disposable `pacing.sqlite3`. A tracked-connection regression requires explicit closure. The rebuilt hash chain, v3 receipts and intentional timing disagreement remain unchanged.

## v0.190.0 exact reviewed account-pacing recovery

`PaperReadPacing.recover_review_block` accepts only an existing blocked journal, its exact current head, the exact bound paper account, a live local ownership lease and two explicit operator confirmations. AUTH_DENIED, unsupported REVIEW_REQUIRED and INTERRUPTED_UNKNOWN tails remain immutable. An interrupted account read now remains review-blocked after its full-day cooldown until recovery is reviewed; the recovery event never shortens `next_allowed_at`, deletes history, changes the retained outcome, reclaims the 256-session cap, reserves admission, retries or continues automatically.

The installed/source `--paper-tool account-recovery` command exposes that bounded mutation without opening credentials, contacting a provider or starting the desktop. Missing/stale/foreign/unconfirmed inputs fail without creating a pacing journal or echoing private arguments. Recovery output keeps provider-wide quota, distributed ownership, routing, live orders, money movement and release authority false. Older binaries do not understand the appended recovery event; preserve a private backup, stop cooperating readers and use the matching or newer reader rather than rewriting or downgrading the journal.

## Historical versions
Earlier development iterations were labeled 1.x, 2.x, and 3.x while the application was still simulation-only. Those labels represented prototype development milestones, **not** production releases or compatibility promises. The development snapshot previously labeled **3.8.0** is now **0.38.0**. Existing Git commits, CI logs, and artifacts retain their original historical names and are not rewritten.

## Forward version policy
- **0.101.0, 0.102.0, 0.103.0, …:** milestone-level pre-release development. Versions may progress beyond 0.101.0 if necessary (e.g., 0.102.0); there is no deadline-based automatic 1.0 promotion.
- **0.x.y:** fixes, small coherent enhancements, and refactoring of that pre-release milestone. No live-money approval implied.
- **1.0.0:** first completed production release, only after all launch acceptance gates are evidenced and explicitly approved by the project owner.
- **1.1.0 onward:** features added after the 1.0.0 launch, maintaining documented compatibility where possible.

The primary implementation sequence is [LAUNCH_DRIVE.md](LAUNCH_DRIVE.md). Global opportunity expansion in [EXPANSION_INITIATIVE.md](EXPANSION_INITIATIVE.md) and secure computer-off operation in [CLOUD_OPERATION.md](CLOUD_OPERATION.md) are explicit staged goals, with data, venue, risk and hosting acceptance before activation. Low-latency autonomous *paper* execution and its reliability tests belong to the v1.0 critical path; real-money use is separately authorized.

## Required gates before 1.0.0
1. Stable packaging, installer, startup, configuration, upgrade and recovery tests.
2. Independently audited and reliable market-data freshness/provenance, gaps, replays and failure behavior.
3. Verified model-provider integrations, safe output validation, cost controls, and no uncontrolled order authority.
4. Chronological historical research using unbiased train/holdout splits, reproducible performance measurements and realistic costs.
5. Sustained autonomous paper-trading reliability with persistent accounting, reconciliation and automatic risk vetoes.
6. Secure, permission-scoped execution infrastructure independently tested with strict order lifecycle handling, idempotency and emergency stops.
7. Extended forward validation, operational monitoring, secrets management, independent security/risk review and documented recovery drills.
8. Secure provider-held funding and governed autonomous execution workflows for the agreed production scope: credential isolation, spendable-funds and cash/position reconciliation, transfer failure/duplicate handling and a separately authorized limited rollout. No release enables deposits or live orders automatically.
9. Secure computer-off operation for the agreed scope, including encrypted durable state, remote stop, distributed ownership and quota/outage recovery.
10. Explicit owner approval for production release and any *separate* authorization for live-money order activation.

A green CI build alone is not a release acceptance gate. Research profitability cannot be guaranteed. The current branch has **no real-money order authority**.

## v0.186.0 guarded account-evidence bundle

`nexus.paper_account_evidence_review.review_with_stability_guards` composes the existing exact-head account-evidence review with the four-observation stability review. The base result must hash-match the guard's embedded accepted reconciliation, account/activity heads must agree, all authority flags stay false and request/activity/pacing/acceptance heads are sampled again before return. The guarded result binds all four observation manifests plus both component hashes. Its current-head variant samples once and never retries; a changed journal fails closed.

The CLI accepts paired `--pre-opening` and `--post-closing` inputs with matching encrypted switches. Supplying only one guard fails before private reads. The guarded public summary exposes bounded diagnostic booleans and counts only, while the full `PAPER_ACCOUNT_EVIDENCE_STABILITY_GUARD_REVIEW` remains private and is rejected by the public-repository audit even when renamed. Sequential local reads still do not prove atomic balances, provider identity, complete received activity, fee links, holds, spendable funds, broker routing, profitability or release readiness. ## v0.184.0 reviewed legacy retired-ID baseline migration

The application-closed order-offload tool now accepts a deliberately private `nexus.order-retired-id-baseline-review.v1` document for a legacy rotated source whose lineage predates the v3 retired-ID fence. The document must explicitly confirm a complete reviewed scope and bind its sorted, unique IDs to the exact source generation, epoch digest, replay digest and verified archive-manifest digest. It is rejected if any client ID is malformed, duplicated, out of order, above the fixed cap, collides at the private fingerprint layer or omits an ID still provable from the current archive.

`review-retired-id-baseline` performs a content-free read-only review. `prepare-replacement --reviewed-retired-id-baseline` then creates a v4 candidate whose replayed lineage binds the aggregate review digest and labels provenance `OPERATOR_REVIEWED_UNATTESTED`. Raw IDs remain only in the caller-selected private file; they are not copied to the candidate, manifest, CLI report or public evidence. Public repository auditing rejects the baseline schema even under an ordinary filename. Existing v1/v2/v3 candidates remain verifiable, and ordinary v3 preparation refuses an unnecessary baseline.

This closes the missing software migration mechanism, not the factual completeness gate. The source cannot attest IDs omitted by older rotations, so a real migration still needs an authorized operator and independent review of the private list. The operation remains halted, flat and non-installing, with broker, funds, live, cloud and release authority false. The read-only roadmap estimate remains about 40% (30–45%, low confidence), with 0/9 fully accepted stages; this bounded migration feature does not justify an estimate increase.

## v0.183.1 Windows write-through rotation publication

Exact v0.183.0 commit `42023862874c0b91249b83e8c90821e59c272291` passed public-security run 39494341943, all safety/evidence/browser jobs in run 39494340579, and Windows regression/EXE/installer run 39494340749. POSIX retains atomic rename followed by parent-directory fsync. Windows calls `MoveFileExW` with `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH`; replacement is requested only where the destination is intentionally superseded. Database and manifest bytes are still flushed separately.

The intent marker remains present across any interrupted pair install, and ordinary gateway access remains blocked until explicit recovery. If marker removal cannot be confirmed, the fence is restored before failure returns. This is a concrete metadata-durability improvement, not proof of behavior through physical power loss, a storage-controller outage, unusual private volumes or ordinary-user ACL policies. Fresh exact-source Windows execution is required. No UI/control, broker connection, retry, resume, source-history deletion, money movement, live order or release authority was added.

## v0.183.0 bounded retired-ID lineage fence

Exact v0.182.2 commit `70e7efdbab7a0cee27da97ef0843c8f74e73d1e3` passed public-security run 39486711199, all safety/evidence/browser jobs in run 39486711203, and Windows regression/EXE/installer run 39486711266. The Windows run completed source binding, 3,435-test regression, Portal syntax, standalone and installed private-tool checks, startup, install/reinstall/uninstall, distribution checksums and retained evidence.

Each new v3 replacement candidate now carries a sorted, bounded private set of 128-bit SHA-256-derived membership fingerprints for all client-order IDs retired across its retained epoch lineage. The lineage and manifest bind the fingerprint policy, exact count and set digest. A reused ID is rejected as `RETIRED_ORDER_ID_REUSE` before a command or linked-ledger mutation. The four-generation fixture proves the set reaches 48 unique retired IDs while every fresh live journal returns to its four-command halted baseline. Existing v1/v2 candidates remain readable. A legacy source already rotated without a complete baseline is not silently upgraded: further replacement preparation fails with `ORDER_OFFLOAD_RETIRED_ORDER_ID_BASELINE_REVIEW_REQUIRED`.

Fingerprints are pseudonymous private operational data, not anonymization; low-entropy IDs may be guessed. They are never emitted in public release evidence or copied to repository fixtures. This local retained-lineage fence does not prove caller identity, coordinate another journal/host/application, attest a broker, or establish broker-wide idempotency. No UI control or parameter changed, and no broker, network, funds, live, cloud or release authority was added. Exact-source v0.183.0 workflows and independent native private-volume/power-loss acceptance remain mandatory.

Final local validation passed 3,443 tests with one existing warning and the final focused set passed 212. ## v0.181.0 replay-bound replacement epoch lineage

Every newly prepared v2 replacement journal adds one durable `bind_replacement_epoch` command after it is initialized, portfolio-linked and halted. The content-free record contains a bounded generation number, the exact predecessor archive-manifest SHA-256 and the prior epoch SHA-256; its own digest is deterministically replayed through ordinary recovery and checkpoints. Candidate, install-recovery and installed-pair verification require these values to agree. A two-rotation regression proves generation 1 → 2 continuity while old v1 candidate, recovery and interrupted-install marker records remain readable.

Lineage does not copy retired order details into the live journal, delete any archive/recovery artifact, prevent arbitrary callers from deliberately reusing an old local simulation ID, resume the runner, connect a provider or grant funds/live/release authority. Full multi-epoch pressure, ID-policy review, native power-loss/private-volume/profile acceptance and exact-source CI remain open. No Portal control or modifiable parameter changed, so the seven tabs and black/bright-purple bottom-right help contract are unchanged.

The packaged-tools harness keeps the ordinary 30-second child limit and widens only the two expensive encrypted-evidence self-tests (observation crypto and sealed quote evidence) to a bounded 60 seconds; crypto parameters and runtime behavior are unchanged. The complete local run passed 3,426 tests before one collection-time generated-roadmap mismatch caused by regenerating the roadmap after import; the frozen-tree roadmap rerun passed 7/7 and the final focused set passed 75 tests. All sixteen profiles and saved-evidence verification matched 347 source files / `d3a47f7e2a034672643ba121afd26c51169d140a756e3ec0d16fa122c030beb4`; `validation/release_trials_0_181_0.json` SHA256 is `ccb06a1c24061d8eb6201af2eb4df1d3ae96b0197bd8acc08761b20089a450a9`. ## v0.177.2 bounded shared-budget contention recovery

Exact v0.177.1 security run 39385781709 and safety/browser/evidence run 39385781778 passed. SQLite writer lock timeouts had been conservatively grouped with expected cap exhaustion.

## v0.177.1 Windows offload handle correction

Exact v0.177.0 public-security run 39335584198 and its browser-help job passed, while the Windows release-evidence operations child failed. Its SQLite integrity connections were entered as transaction context managers, which do not close the underlying handles. Linux permitted rename/removal with the leaked handles, masking the native locking defect. v0.177.1 explicitly closes both read-only integrity connections before replay, rename and cleanup; a regression blocks publication until every tracked integrity handle is closed.

This patch changes no command semantics, user interface, editable parameter, permission, risk limit, account, provider, funding, live/cloud behavior or authority flag. Exact-source Windows regression/release evidence, standalone/installed command smoke, packaging/installer and security workflows remain mandatory.

## v0.176.0 sustained-trial capacity readiness

The existing read-only Paper Trial Readiness action now combines retained quote/session headroom with the replayed durable order journal's ordinary-command and intent-ID capacity. It reports whether the current reserve can admit the next runner cycle and separately computes a conservative upper bound that assumes every policy-cadence cycle consumes the runner's full admission reserve. That bound is a fail-closed capacity diagnostic, not predicted strategy activity, expected orders, performance or market realism.

Missing histories remain absent and no check starts the engine, creates a session, reserves storage, opens credentials, contacts a provider or changes an account. The view keeps all authority flags false. A 72-hour quote window fits the current 100,000-row evidence cap on a fresh profile, while its deliberately conservative order bound exceeds current command/ID limits and therefore makes the physical-offload/long-run gate visible instead of implying sustained readiness. Exact-source Windows, browser/help, release-evidence and security CI remain required.

## v0.177.0 verified physical-copy foundation

The new `order-offload` installed/source tool requires an explicit application-closed confirmation and refuses anything except a stopped, flat, reconciled journal with at least one logical archive batch and no active command epoch. It rejects SQLite sidecars, symlinks, hard links, special files, missing linked portfolio state, oversized inputs, an existing or in-tree destination and malformed/tampered output. It stages a new private directory, copies both SQLite databases, records content hashes and bounded replay counts, runs integrity checks plus complete archived-order replay, and verifies that the source bytes/replay remained unchanged before and after publication.

The output is deliberately copy-only. Source deletion and `capacity_reclaimed` remain false; there is no compaction, rotation policy, automatic resume, broker access, credential access, order submission or permission change. The operations release profile now includes a disposable verified-copy fixture, and the packaged tool smoke runs create and verify through the executable command dispatcher. See `ORDER_OFFLOAD.md` for operator scope. No Portal button or editable parameter was added, so the seven tabs and existing black/bright-purple beginner-help contract are unchanged.

Exact-source v0.177.0 Windows packaging/installed-tool, safety/browser/evidence and public-security workflows remain mandatory, as do private-volume/native-profile recovery acceptance, multi-epoch rotation/compaction, sustained long-run trials, actual broker-paper reconciliation and every funding/live/cloud/independent/owner gate.

## Enforcement
Application version, package metadata and Windows installer version/output filename must match. Automated tests guard against accidentally labeling an unfinished preview 1.0.0 or 1.1.0. The hourly autonomous development task is instructed to keep all work within the 0.x prerelease series unless these gates are independently evidenced and the user explicitly directs the promotion.
